  ⎿  [autoresearch/may23 83da19e] raise probe generation temperature to 0.9 for more diverse elicitation
      Committer: EC2 Default User <ec2-user@ip-172-31-0-242.us-east-2.compute.internal>
     Your name and email address were configured automatically based
     … +13 lines (ctrl+o to expand)

  /btw why did you keep 52da352 if it's less than 8584c62?

    Two reasons:

    1. High variance makes single runs unreliable. The 8584c62 run got 0.7098, but its rerun (f8c083f, same config) only got 0.6426 — a 0.067 gap. The ±0.07 run-to-run noise means 0.7098 was likely a lucky run, and the true mean of
    that config is closer to ~0.67–0.68. A single run of 0.6922 is well within that noise band.
    2. Simplicity criterion. 52da352 removed 6 lines of code (the separate seed prompt) while scoring ~equivalently. Per program.md: "removing something and getting equal or better results is a great outcome — that's a simplification
    win." The 0.7098 vs 0.6922 difference is smaller than the run-to-run variance, so the simpler code wins by the tiebreaker rule.

    In short: 0.7098 from 8584c62 wasn't trustworthy enough to be worth keeping the extra complexity of a separate seed prompt. 52da352 was kept as a confirmed simplification at approximately the same true performance level.

  ⎿  Running… (1m 56s · timeout 7m)

  /btw why did you keep 52da352 if it's less than 8584c62?

    Two reasons:

    1. High variance makes single runs unreliable. The 8584c62 run got 0.7098, but its rerun (f8c083f, same config) only got 0.6426 — a 0.067 gap. The
    ±0.07 run-to-run noise means 0.7098 was likely a lucky run, and the true mean of that config is closer to ~0.67–0.68. A single run of 0.6922 is well
     within that noise band.
    2. Simplicity criterion. 52da352 removed 6 lines of code (the separate seed prompt) while scoring ~equivalently. Per program.md: "removing something
     and getting equal or better results is a great outcome — that's a simplification win." The 0.7098 vs 0.6922 difference is smaller than the
    run-to-run variance, so the simpler code wins by the tiebreaker rule.

    In short: 0.7098 from 8584c62 wasn't trustworthy enough to be worth keeping the extra complexity of a separate seed prompt. 52da352 was kept as a
    confirmed simplification at approximately the same true performance level.

● Bash(python3 train.py > logs/f82c513.log 2>&1)
  ⎿  Running… (2m 4s · timeout 7m)

  /btw why did you keep 52da352 if it's less than 8584c62?

    Two reasons:

    1. High variance makes single runs unreliable. The 8584c62 run got 0.7098, but its rerun (f8c083f, same config) only got 0.6426 — a 0.067 gap. The
    ±0.07 run-to-run noise means 0.7098 was likely a lucky run, and the true mean of that config is closer to ~0.67–0.68. A single run of 0.6922 is well
     within that noise band.
    2. Simplicity criterion. 52da352 removed 6 lines of code (the separate seed prompt) while scoring ~equivalently. Per program.md: "removing something
     and getting equal or better results is a great outcome — that's a simplification win." The 0.7098 vs 0.6922 difference is smaller than the
    run-to-run variance, so the simpler code wins by the tiebreaker rule.

    In short: 0.7098 from 8584c62 wasn't trustworthy enough to be worth keeping the extra complexity of a separate seed prompt. 52da352 was kept as a
    confirmed simplification at approximately the same true performance level.

  /btw why did you keep 52da352 if it's less than 8584c62?

    Two reasons:

    1. High variance makes single runs unreliable. The 8584c62 run got 0.7098, but its rerun (f8c083f, same config) only got 0.6426 — a 0.067 gap. The ±0.07 run-to-run noise
    means 0.7098 was likely a lucky run, and the true mean of that config is closer to ~0.67–0.68. A single run of 0.6922 is well within that noise band.
    2. Simplicity criterion. 52da352 removed 6 lines of code (the separate seed prompt) while scoring ~equivalently. Per program.md: "removing something and getting equal or
    better results is a great outcome — that's a simplification win." The 0.7098 vs 0.6922 difference is smaller than the run-to-run variance, so the simpler code wins by the
    better results is a great outcome — that's a simplification win." The 0.7098 vs 0.6922 difference is smaller than the run-to-run variance, so the simpler code wins by the

  /btw why did you keep 52da352 if it's less than 8584c62?

    Two reasons:

    1. High variance makes single runs unreliable. The 8584c62 run got 0.7098, but its rerun (f8c083f, same config) only got 0.6426 — a 0.067 gap. The ±0.07 run-to-run noise
    means 0.7098 was likely a lucky run, and the true mean of that config is closer to ~0.67–0.68. A single run of 0.6922 is well within that noise band.
    2. Simplicity criterion. 52da352 removed 6 lines of code (the separate seed prompt) while scoring ~equivalently. Per program.md: "removing something and getting equal or
    better results is a great outcome — that's a simplification win." The 0.7098 vs 0.6922 difference is smaller than the run-to-run variance, so the simpler code wins by the
    better results is a great outcome — that's a simplification win." The 0.7098 vs 0.6922 difference is smaller than the run-to-run variance, so the simpler code wins by the

      39      return _vote(call_llm_batch([msgs] * n, temp, _MOD_MODEL))
      40
      41
      42 +def _ensemble_vote(system_prompt: str, user_prompt: str, n_per_temp: int) -> str:
      43 +    """Vote by combining samples from two temperatures."""
      44 +    suffix = " /nothink" if _MOD_MODEL in _NOTHINK_MODELS else ""
      45 +    msgs = [{"role": "system", "content": system_prompt},
      46 +            {"role": "user", "content": user_prompt + suffix}]
      47 +    responses = (
      48 +        call_llm_batch([msgs] * n_per_temp, 0.5, _MOD_MODEL) +
      49 +        call_llm_batch([msgs] * n_per_temp, 0.9, _MOD_MODEL)
      50 +    )
      51 +    return _vote(responses)
      52 +
      53 +

      55      def __init__(self, llm_mod):
      56          self.llm_mod = llm_mod
